8.1.1.1.

Study Results

Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
Data:
This study was designed and monitored in accordance with sponsor procedures, which comply
with the ethical principles of Good Clinical Practice (GCP) as required by the major regulatory
authorities, and in accordance with the Declaration of Helsinki.
The Applicant’s Position:
Study MEK-NF-201 is GCP compliant.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA acknowledges the Applicant’s statement of compliance with GCP requirements.
Data Quality and Integrity
Data:
All participant data relating to the study were entered into the electronic case report forms
(eCRFs) unless transmitted to the sponsor or designee electronically (e.g., laboratory data).
The investigator was responsible for verifying that data entries were accurate and correct by
electronically signing the eCRF. The investigator maintained accurate documentation (source
data) that supported the information entered in the eCRF. The investigator permitted studyrelated monitoring, audits, Institutional review board (IRB)/Independent ethics committee (IEC)
review, and regulatory agency inspections and provided direct access to source data
documents.
Monitoring details describing strategy methods, responsibilities, and requirements, including
handling of noncompliance issues and monitoring techniques (central, remote, or on-site
monitoring) were indicated in the monitoring plan to ensure the protocol and GCP was
followed.
The sponsor or designee was responsible for the data management of this study including
quality checking of the data. The sponsor assumed accountability for actions delegated to other
individuals (e.g., Contract Research Organizations).
Study monitors performed ongoing source data verification to confirm that data entered into
the eCRF by authorized site personnel were accurate, complete, and verifiable from source
documents; that the safety and rights of participants were being protected; and that the study
was being conducted in accordance with the currently approved protocol and any other study
agreements, International Conference on Harmonization (ICH) GCP, and all applicable
regulatory requirements.

Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
Records and documents, including signed ICFs, pertaining to the conduct of this study were
retained by the investigator according to specifications in the ICH guidelines, local regulations,
or as specified in the clinical trial agreement, whichever was longer. No records were to be
destroyed during the retention period without the written approval of the sponsor. No records
were to be transferred to another location or party without written notification to the sponsor.
SpringWorks conducts clinical studies according to procedures that incorporate the ethical
principles of GCP. To ensure compliance with these procedures and to assess the adequacy of
quality control procedures, SpringWorks undertakes a GCP audit program.
Audits were performed by SpringWorks Quality Assurance (or designees) operating
independently of the study monitors. The audits within the clinical program were aimed at
study documentation, investigator sites, and clinical study reports (CSRs).
The audit program, together with SpringWorks’ internal quality control procedures, provide
reassurance that study conclusions were based on valid procedures for data management and
analysis, and that the clinical study program was carried out in accordance with GCP guidelines.
The Applicant’s Position:
The study conduct, quality and integrity were appropriate to allow adequate assessment of the
data.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA did not identify quality or integrity issues in the data. The data submitted were organized
and adequate to perform a complete review of the efficacy of mirdametinib.
Financial Disclosure
The Applicant’s Position:
All investigators have been researched for conflicts of interest. None were identified as having
financial interests/arrangements with the sponsor.
The FDA’s Assessment:
In accordance with 21 CFR Part 54, the Applicant submitted Form FDA 3454 (Financial Interests
and Arrangements of Clinical Investigators) certifying that as the sponsor of the submitted
studies, they did not enter into any financial arrangement with the listed clinical investigators
whereby the value of compensation to the investigator could be affected by the outcome of the
study. All investigators were assessed for equity interest, significant payments of other sorts,
and other compensation by the sponsor or proprietary interest. The Applicant certified that
none of the financial interests or arrangements described in 21 CFR Part 54 existed for any of
the investigators who participated in Study MEK-NF-201. The Applicant provided a list of all
investigators for Study MEK-NF-201 in Appendix 16.1.4 of the CSR.

Abbreviations: AE: adverse event; FAS: Full Analysis Set; PD: progressive disease; PPS: Per-protocol Set; LTFU:
Long-term Follow-up.
The Safety Analysis Set and FAS was defined as the set of all participants who took at least one dose of
mirdametinib.
The PPS was defined as the set of all participants in the FAS who completed at least one post-baseline disease
evaluation and who did not have any major CSR-reportable deviations, including but not limited to efficacy
assessments or compliance.
Participants were considered to have completed the Treatment Phase if they completed the Cycle 24 visit (End of
Treatment Phase) or have entered the LTFU Phase.
Percentages were calculated out of the number of participants eligible for LTFU Phase for ‘entering LTFU
Phase/did not enter LTFU Phase’ and reasons for not entering, and out of all participants in the cohort for all
other rows.

External Control Data
Participant baseline disease characteristics were generally similar between MEK-NF-201 and
external control data included for comparison. As Study 01-C-022 only included pediatric
participants with a progressive PN, these data were compared against pediatric participants
with progressive PN in Study MEK-NF-201 to provide the most accurate comparison. All adult
and pediatric participants in Study MEK-NF-201 were compared against adult and pediatric
participants enrolled in the external Study 08-C-0079, but disease severity and rate of
progression in the external controls were not known, which is a limitation to this evaluation.
For comparisons to natural history data, only participants in Study MEK-NF-201 who had both a
baseline and post-baseline scan were included for analysis. The Study MEK-NF-201 cohorts for
comparison were:
x
x
x

Pediatric participants with progressive PN: n=34
Pediatric Participants: n=54
Adult participants: n=50.

The Applicant’s Position:
Disease characteristics were consistent with the disease under study.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA generally agrees that the baseline disease characteristics of patients in the ReNeu study
are consistent with the general population of patients with NF1-PN
The FDA views the external control data as supportive in a descriptive manner. The data from
Studies 01-C-022 and 08-C-0079 were not independently confirmed and will not be included in
FDA’s assessment of the efficacy results for the ReNeu study.

Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
Data:
Participant compliance with study treatment was assessed at each visit where study treatment
was dispensed. Compliance was assessed by counting returned capsules/tablets in addition to
reviewing the dosing diary entries from the home electronic patient-reported outcome (ePRO)
device used in the Treatment Phase (participants were trained on the home ePRO device at the
Screening Visit). Study treatment exposure is presented in Table 38.

Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
Yearly Percentage Change from Baseline in Target PN Compared to External Control Data
For efficacy analyses, one-to-one propensity score matching was used as the balancing method.
After one-to-one propensity score matching, variance ratios became within the recommended
range of 0.5 to 2, which supports that the groups are well balanced with respect to the selected
demographic characteristics (Rubin 2001).
In Cohort 1 (Study MEK-NF-201 pediatric participants with progressive PN and Study 01-C-0222
pediatric participants with progressive PN), participants in Study MEK-NF-201 had a significant
decrease in yearly percentage change in the target PN volume while participants from Study 01C-0222 had increases in PN volume at all the time intervals. After adjusting for potential
differences in baselines covariates using one-to-one propensity score matching, the LS mean
percentage differences (standard errors) were -43.72 (8.816), -23.76 (5.812), -21.91 (7.874),
and -38,72 (8.331), at Year 1, 2, 3, and the last assessment, respectively. This improvement
remained significant and robust even after a sensitivity analysis comparing alternative target
lesion at baseline.
In Cohort 2 (Study MEK-NF-201 pediatric participants and Study 08-C-0079 pediatric
participants), participants in Study MEK-NF-201 had a significant decrease in yearly percentage
change in the target PN volume while participants from Study 08-C-0079 had increases in PN
volume at all time intervals. After adjusting for potential differences in baselines covariates
using one-to-one propensity score matching, the LS mean percentage differences (standard
errors) were -40.63 (6.699), -36.62 (3.891), -27.53 (4.471), and -24.28 (5.701), at Year 1, 2, 3,
and the last assessment, respectively.
In Cohort 3 (Study MEK-NF-201 adult participants and Study 08-C-0079 adult participants),
participants in Study MEK-NF-201 had a substantial decrease in yearly percentage change in the
target PN volume as compared to an increase in the yearly percentage change in the target PN
volume in participants from Study 08-C-0079 at all-time intervals. All results were statistically
ƐŝŐŶŝĨŝĐĂŶƚ ĞǆĐĞƉƚ ĨŽƌ zĞĂƌ ϯ ĂĨƚĞƌ ƉƌŽƉĞŶƐŝƚǇ ƐĐŽƌĞ ŵĂƚĐŚŝŶŐ͘ ^ŝŶĐĞ ŽŶůǇ ϯϴй ŽĨ ƉĂƌƚŝĐŝƉĂŶƚƐ
from Study MEK-NF-201 were matched with participants from Study 08-C-0079 after one-toone propensity score matching without replacement, treatment comparisons based on this
matching result need to be interpreted with caution. After adjusting for potential differences in
baselines covariates, using IPTW, the LS mean percentage differences (standard errors)
were -26.74 (6.470), -24.96 (4.628), -19.79 (5.067), and -27.14 (6.189), at Year 1, 2, 3 and the
last assessment, respectively.

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the results for ORR presented in this section, noting that as a single arm trial
with no control for multiplicity in any of the hypothesis tests presented in Table 39 and Table
40, the p-values reported by the Applicant should be interpreted with caution. In addition,
there were no complete responses which should be acknowledged when describing responses
as “deep.”
The primary endpoint is defined as confirmed ORR at the end of the treatment phase, using
centrally read MRI volumetric analysis, where a confirmed response is specified as a PN
ĚĞĐƌĞĂƐĞ ŽĨ шϮϬй ĐŽŵƉĂƌĞĚ ƚŽ ďĂƐĞůŝŶĞ ŽŶ ĐŽŶƐĞĐƵƚŝǀĞ ƐĐĂŶƐ ǁŝƚŚŝŶ Ϯ-6 months. This differs
from the REiNS criteria for partial response, which defines confirmed PR based on consecutive
scans within 3-6 months. The rationale for this modification was primarily to account for
patients in whom a first response observed at Cycle 21 would be confirmed at Cycle 24 (end of
Treatment Phase) which, per protocol, could occur at 3 months +/- 10 days. Ultimately, three
adult patients had a first response at Cycle 21 with a confirmatory scan at 91, 91, and 86 days,
falling short of strict REiNs criteria. All three patients continued to have a confirmed response
in the long-term follow-up phase. Two patients, one adult, and one pediatric, had unscheduled
MRI assessments which led to confirmatory scan intervals of 70 days. The adult patient had an
MRI assessment at the scheduled time; however, it was missing the PD/T2 sequence; because
this sequence was required according to the Imaging Review Charter v8.0, an unscheduled MRI
was conducted at a later date to meet study imaging criteria for volumetric evaluation of the
target PN. This patient went on to have progressive disease. The pediatric patient had an

Persistence of Effect
Data:
Refer to efficacy results – secondary and other relevant endpoints above.
The Applicant’s Position:
The study demonstrated persistence of effect over time based on the observed duration of
response.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA generally agrees with the Applicant, adding that with the original data submission no
deaths were observed in the pediatric cohort and only one death was observed in the adult
cohort. These findings remained the same with no additional deaths or progressions with the
90-day safety update report.

assessed by PedsQL, pain severity as assessed by NRS-11, and pain interference as assessed by
PII, and were assessed at the prespecified Cycle 13 timepoint (Figure 11):

of life.

from baseline in patient-reported NRS-11 and patient/parent proxy-reported PII, beginning

participants a significant improvement in PedsQL total score, durable and significant reductions
from baseline in NRS-11 and PII, beginning early and sustained throughout the cycles, were
observed.
Additionally, meaningful change threshold (MCT) analyses were performed for each PRO
assessment to further describe clinical benefit of mirdametinib using a distribution -based
approach and generally demonstrated that the statistically significant PRO findings were
associated with clinically meaningful improvements.

Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
Study NF-106 was an investigator-initiated, open-label, multi-center, Phase 2 study to evaluate
ƐĂĨĞƚǇ ĂŶĚ ĞĨĨŝĐĂĐǇ ŽĨ ŵŝƌĚĂŵĞƚŝŶŝď ŝŶ ƉĂƌƚŝĐŝƉĂŶƚƐ шϭϲ ǇĞĂƌƐ ŽůĚ ǁŝƚŚ E&ϭ ĂŶĚ ƐǇŵƉƚŽŵĂƚŝĐ Žƌ
progressing PN. This study has been completed. The primary endpoint was confirmed ORR
based on volumetric MRI analysis. Key secondary objectives included evaluation of QoL and
pain, characterization of the single dose PK profile of mirdametinib in the NF1 population, and
the evaluation of safety and tolerability of chronic mirdametinib administration. Mirdametinib
was orally administered at a dose of 2 mg/m2 BID (maximum 4 mg BID) in 4-week cycles on an
intermittent (3 weeks on/1 week off) schedule, for up to 24 cycles. Participants were required
to have шϭϱй ƌĞĚƵĐƚŝŽŶ ŝŶ ƚĂrget tumor volume at the end of Cycle 8 of therapy to continue to
receive treatment up to Cycle 12. If a participant ŚĂĚ шϮϬй ƌĞĚƵĐƚŝŽŶ ŝŶ ƚĂƌŐĞƚ ƚƵŵŽƌ ǀŽůƵŵĞ at
the end of Cycle 12 of therapy, they could continue on study up to Cycle 24. Evaluations were
performed every 4 cycles of treatment for the first year, followed by every 6 cycles thereafter.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The NF-106 study is a supportive study, and the efficacy results were not verified by FDA. The
efficacy portion of the label for marketing application is solely based on the MEK-NF-201 study
results.

Secondary Efficacy Endpoints
Duration of Response
A secondary efficacy objective was to determine the DoR for participants who attained a best
overall confirmed response of CR or PR using MRI volumetric analysis. The DoR was defined as
the time (in months) between the first instance of response that was subsequently confirmed,
until the date of radiographic disease progression or death, whichever occurred first.
In the absence of radiographic progression or death, participants had their results censored to
the date of their most adequate tumor assessment date prior to the start of any surgery to the
target PN or the start of any anti-cancer/PN medication (e.g., chemotherapy, immunology,
biologic therapy, or radiation therapy).
DoR was analyzed using the Kaplan-Meier method based on participants with a confirmed CR or
WZ͘ ĚĚŝƚŝŽŶĂůůǇ͕ ƚŚĞ <D ĞƐƚŝŵĂƚĞƐ ǁŝƚŚ ĐŽƌƌĞƐƉŽŶĚŝŶŐ ϵϱй /Ɛ ;ƵƐŝŶŐ ƚŚĞ ůŽŐ-log
transformation) and number of participants at risk will be estimated at the following time
points: 2, 4, 8, 12, 18, and 24 months.
Time to response was summarized descriptively for confirmed responders and focused on the
evaluation of outcomes within each treatment arm.
Patient Reported Outcomes
Absolute values and changes from baseline in NRS-11 scores were summarized. Summaries by
ORR status (i.e., participants with confirmed objective response herein denoted as ‘responders’
versus participants who did not achieve confirmed objective response or ‘non-responders’)
were provided.
Additionally, a MMRM was fit for overall and adult participants only with change from baseline
in NRS-11 score as the dependent variable, visit as a fixed effect and baseline NRS-11 score as a
covariate. Covariance structures were trialed in the order of decreasing complexity, with the
first structure which converges without error used. The structures tested were as follows:
Unstructured, Heterogenous Toeplitz, Heterogenous Autoregressive, Autoregressive,
Heterogenous Compound Symmetry, Compound Symmetry, Variance Components.
Absolute values and changes from baseline in BPI-Pain Interference Subscale scores were
summarized descriptively by visit for the corresponding domains and total scores. Summaries
by ORR status (responder versus non-responder) were also presented.

Study Results

Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
Data:
This study was designed and monitored in accordance with sponsor procedures, which comply
with the ethical principles of GCP as required by the major regulatory authorities.
The Applicant’s Position:
Study NF-106 is GCP compliant.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA acknowledges the Applicant’s statement of compliance with GCP requirements.
Data Quality and Integrity
Data:
The study investigator and clinical coordinator reviewed the study progress regularly.
Participants entered on the study and AEs were reviewed to ensure that the study was
conducted as outlined in the protocol. Monthly reports were generated by the NF Consortium
to assess completeness of data. There were monthly phone conferences between the NF
Consortium and the investigator to address quality assurance issues. A Data Safety Monitoring
Board was established for the purpose of ensuring data compliance and regular monitoring of
the study.
The Applicant’s Position:
No issues were identified that could potentially impact data integrity, prevent an adequate
assessment of the data, or change the conclusions drawn.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA did not identify quality or integrity issues in the data. The data submitted were organized
and adequate to perform a complete review of mirdametinib.

the integrity of the study data was not affected by the financial interest of the investigators.

Dose/Dose Response
The Applicant’s Position:
Mirdametinib was dosed in Studies MEK-NF-201 and NF-106 by BSA. The median (minimum,
maximum) of actual doses received by participants with NF1 PN was 2.21 mg/m2 (0.806, 2.86)
(Figure 4).
In addition, population and trial simulations were conducted using exposure-efficacy and
exposure-safety models to predict the impact of alternative target dose levels from 1.0 to 3.0
mg/m2 BID (1.0, 1.5, 2.0, and 3.0 mg/m2). The simulations support that exposures associated
with the proposed marketing dose of 2 mg/m2 of mirdametinib provide an optimal balance of

At Cycle 12, 9 participants completed the BPI. There were improvements found in the BPI with

from baseline at Cycle 12 in PedsQL NF1 module total score was 3.6 (-20, 29), indicating an
improvement in NF1-related QoL. All 6 responders completed Cycle 12.
At Cycle 24, 4 participants completed the PedsQL NF1 module. The median (min, max) change
from baseline at Cycle 24 in PedsQL NF1 total score was 15.6 (-8, 34), indicating an
improvement in NF1-related QoL.

Review of Safety
Safety Review Approach
Data:
See the Applicant’s Position below.
The Applicant’s Position:
The pivotal safety data supporting the safety evaluation of mirdametinib is based on Study
MEK-NF-201. The studies used to support the integrated summary of safety are described in
Table 26. Data from 8 additional non pooled interventional studies and the compassionate use
program were used as supportive safety information, for a total of 577 patients exposed to
mirdametinib.
As agreed with the agency during the Pre-NDA meeting, the integrated safety data presents 4
clinical studies that were grouped into the following study pools based on the targeted
underlying condition (NF1 PN or advanced cancers), dosing regimen (body surface area vs unit
dosing), participants, and study design:
x

Pool 1: Study MEK-NF-201 and Study NF-106 in adult and pediatric participants with NF1
PN. The data from these studies were analyzed together as a single pool and are the
primary safety data used to assess the safety profile of mirdametinib in adult and
pediatric participants with NF1 PN.

x

Pool 2: Study A4581001 and Study A4581002 in adult participants with advanced
cancers. The data from these studies were analyzed together as a single pool and are
supportive safety data used to assess the safety profile of mirdametinib.

This Assessment Aid will provide the safety data from Study MEK-NF-201, followed by Pool 1
and Pool 2, and supported by relevant data from healthy volunteers and the Global
Pharmacovigilance database. Data will be compared to the MEK-NF-201 data as it serves as the
primary basis for establishing the efficacy of mirdametinib in the treatment of participants with
NF1 PN.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s description of the pivotal and supportive datasets included in
the NDA to support the safety evaluation of mirdametinib. FDA independently reviewed
exposure and safety data for the pivotal ReNeu study and supportive Study NF-106 in which
safety data was analyzed together as Pool 1 to support the safety profile for adult and pediatric
patients with NF1 PN treated with mirdametinib 2 mg/m2 BID (maximum 4 mg BID); 3 weeks
on/1 week off.

210
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5528369

NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
A 90-day updated safety analysis with corresponding datasets for Pool 1 (data cut-off date of
September 19, 2024) was submitted to supplement the summary of clinical safety. The 90-day
safety update included an additional 9 months of safety data. There were no new patients
enrolled into Pool 1 since the initial data cut (Primary Analysis). Study NF-106 was completed
prior to the primary analysis and there was no additional data in the 90-day safety update. The
90-day safety update did not identify new safety signals not already discussed in this review.
FDA reviewed but did not independently verify the data from Pool 2 which included Study
A4581001 and Study A4581002 in adult patients with advanced cancers.
Review of the Safety Database
Overall Exposure
Data:
A total of 577 patients have received at least one dose of mirdametinib across the clinical
program.
A total of 56 participants in the pediatric cohort of Study MEK-NF-201 received at least 1 dose
of study treatment. The median (min, max) duration of study treatment exposure was 22.0 (1.6,
40.0) months. A total of 58 participants in the adult cohort received at least 1 dose of study
treatment. The median (min, max) duration of study treatment exposure was 21.8 (0.4, 45.6)
months.
The exposure to mirdametinib in Pool 1 aligns with what was observed in the pivotal study,
MEK-NF-201. In Pool 1, a total of 133 participants (58 participants in the pediatric cohort and
75 participants in the adult cohort) received at least one dose of study treatment. As displayed
in Table 48, the median (min, max) duration of mirdametinib exposure for the pediatric cohort
was 21.87 (1.6, 40.1) months and 18.66 (0.4, 45.6) months in the adult cohort of Pool 1. The
median duration of study treatment, median duration to dose reduction and interruption were
similar between the pediatric and adult cohorts. The median time to dose modification was
slightly longer in the pediatric cohort at 4.2 (range: 0 to 21.6) months compared to the adult

Adequacy of the safety database:
Data:
Refer to Table 15 for the number of participants enrolled in each study used to support the
evaluation of safety for mirdametinib.
The Applicant’s Position:
The evaluation of safety of mirdametinib for the treatment of participants with NF1 PN is based
on data from Study MEK-NF-201 and Pool 1 (Studies MEK-NF-201 and NF-106), which comprises
both adult and pediatric cohorts (N=133).
Supportive safety information for mirdametinib as a monotherapy was generated by pooling
two studies conducted in adult patients with advanced cancer (A4581001 and A4581002)
(N=113). Due to the differences in dosing and patient populations these were pooled separately
from the studies in participants with NF1 PN (Pool 2).
Additional supportive safety data from 3 Phase 1 studies in which healthy volunteers received
single doses of mirdametinib (88 patients received mirdametinib in these three studies) as well
as data from other interventional studies and compassionate use programs were used to
support the safety of mirdametinib. A total of 577 patients have received at least one dose of
mirdametinib which is considered adequate to define the risks of mirdametinib treatment in
this rare disease.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment of the adequacy of the safety database to support
review of the NDA.

treatment is taken, including those occurring or increasing in severity up to 30 days after the
last dose of study treatment for Pool 1. TEAEs for Pool 2 were defined as all AEs reported after

and up to 30 days after the last dose of study treatment.
TEAE severity was graded by NCI CTCAE (Version 5.0 for Pool 1 and Version 3.0 for Pool 2).
To facilitate a better understanding of specific topics of interest that may have comprised
multiple PTs, select TEAEs were evaluated by pre-defined categories.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s description of AE categorization; however, FDA does not
consider relatedness as part of the safety review of AEs. The FDA review was completed using
MedRA preferred terms (PTs) and CTCAE Version 5.0 as well as custom grouped terms (GT)
when performing independent analyses of AEs for Pool 1.

The Applicant’s Position:
The incidence of AEs leading to dose modification (interruption and/or reduction) was similar in
Study MEK-NF-201, Pool 1, and Pool 2. The most common AEs leading to dose modification of
mirdametinib in patients with NF1 PN were consistent with the known safety profile of
mirdametinib or were commonly reported during the global pandemic.

NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
Adverse drug reactions (ADRs) for mirdametinib are defined as those AEs considered related to
mirdametinib based on the assessment of numerical frequency data and review of individual
case details such as temporal association of the event with administration of mirdametinib,
dechallenge and rechallenge information, and confounding factors such as intercurrent
illnesses and administration of concomitant medications which could be plausible alternative
causes for the reported events. The presence of a description of a plausible mechanism by
which MEK inhibition could lead to the reported event in the literature was also taken into
account when considering if a reported event was an adverse reaction for mirdametinib.
Based on the totality of the data, AEs that are considered ADRs for mirdametinib in Study MEKNF-201 and Pool 1 are presented in Table 56 and Table 57, respectively, which forms the basis
for the ADR information included in the prescribing information.
The data described below reflect exposure to mirdametinib in participants with NF1 PN in Study
MEK-NF-201 (N=114) or pooled across studies MEK-NF-201 and NF-106 in Pool 1 (N=133).
Serious ADRs, permanent discontinuation of mirdametinib due to an ADR, dose interruptions
due to ADRs and dose reductions due to ADRs are described below. Note that individual
participants could have reported more than 1 event leading to a dose modification or dose
modifications, or as reasons for treatment discontinuation.
Study MEK-NF-201
No serious ADRs occurred in pediatric participants who received mirdametinib. Serious ADRs

The Applicant’s Position:
The overall TEAE profile in Pool 1 was similar to what was observed in Study MEK-NF-201.
Overall, the TEAE profile between the pediatric and adult cohorts of Pool 1 was similar. There
were more participants in the adult cohort that reported TEAEs that led to study

participants in the pediatric cohort).
Overall, the incidence of TEAEs in Pool 1 was similar to what was seen in the pivotal study,
MEK-NF-201. dŚĞ ŵŽƐƚ ĨƌĞƋƵĞŶƚůǇ ;хϯϱйͿ ƌĞƉŽƌƚĞĚ dƐ ĨŽƌ ƚŚĞ ƚŽƚĂů ƉŽƉƵůĂƚŝŽŶ ĨƌŽŵ ^ƚƵĚǇ
MEK-NF-201 and Pool 1 were dermatitis acneiform, diarrhea, nausea, and vomiting. The most
frequently reported PTs were expected with MEK inhibition (Subbiah 2020, Solares 2021).
The following events are recognized as risks for patients with NF1 PN treated with
mirdametinib 2 mg/m2 BID:
x The following events were recognized as identified risks because of the frequency of
their reports, temporal relationship to the start of treatment with mirdametinib, and
presence of a plausible biologic mechanism, and were deemed important due to their
potential impact on patients: ejection fraction decreased and retinal vein occlusion.
Given their importance, these risks are recommended for inclusion in Warnings and
Precautions.
x The following events were reported at an increased incidence relative to that
anticipated for the patient population with NF1 PN and are recognized as non-important
identified risks as these events were generally Grade 1 or 2 and did not frequently result
in the discontinuation of study treatment: diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, abdominal pain,
constipation, skin events (including dermatitis acneiform, rash, pruritus, dry skin,
alopecia, hair color changes, and hair texture), blood CPK increased, alanine
aminotransferase increased, aspartate aminotransferase increased, blood alkaline
phosphatase increased, and fatigue.
o While dermatitis acneiform and rash are considered non-important identified
risks, they are proposed for inclusion in the Warnings and Precautions due to
recommended dose modifications for severe events.
x The data presented support recognizing stomatitis as a non-important identified risk for
mirdametinib in the pediatric population and as a non-important potential risk in the
adult population.
x The data presented support recognizing paronychia, eczema, and neutrophil count
decreased, as non-important identified risks for mirdametinib in the pediatric
population.
x Given the low frequency and severity of events, the data support vision blurred and
detachment of retinal pigment epithelium being recognized as important potential risks.
Given their importance, these risks are recommended for inclusion in Warnings and

Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)

Laboratory Findings
Data:
Refer to Table 58 and Table 59.
The Applicant’s Position:
The data supports recognizing blood CPK increased, AST increased, ALT increased, and blood
alkaline phosphatase increased as non-important identified risks for mirdametinib, neutrophil
count decreased as a non-important identified risk for pediatric patients receiving
mirdametinib, and ejection fraction decreased as an important identified risk for mirdametinib.
Of these identified risks, only ejection fraction decreased is assessed as being an important
identified risk and is therefore proposed for inclusion in Warnings and Precautions. While the
measured decreases in ejection fraction were asymptomatic in study participants, the potential
clinical impact of an undetected decrease in ejection fraction is not known, hence this risk is
considered important. Blood CPK increased is considered a non-important identified risk in view
of the lack of reports of serious events, including no reports of rhabdomyolysis. AST and ALT
increased are considered non-important identified risks due to the infrequency of Grade 3 shifts
and dose modifications. Blood alkaline phosphatase is considered a non-important identified
risk due to the absence of correlation with other markers of liver injury in Pool 1. Neutrophil
count decreased is recognized as a non-important identified risk for mirdametinib in pediatric
participants only given the infrequent reporting of the event in the adult cohort, and the
absence of risks of opportunistic infections to participants reporting or observed to have
decreased neutrophils.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Laboratory tests to assess hematology and chemistries parameters were measured at baseline,
followed by Day 15 during every Cycle. The reported percentages were calculated using the
number of patients with baseline and post-baseline results for each respective parameter.

The overall data suggests that changes in vital signs during treatment with mirdametinib is

Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
Data:
ECG parameters were not collected in Study NF-106 and therefore observations concerning
ECGs came from Study MEK-NF-201 only.
In Study MEK-NF-201, in the pediatric cohort, there were no major changes observed in ECG
parameters. In the adult cohort, several participants had QTcF between 450 and 480 msec
throughout the study and 1 participant had a QTcF >480 msec on Cycle 5 Day 15. One TEAE of
Grade 1 electrocardiogram QT prolonged was reported; this participant did not report a QTcF
>450 msec. There were no major changes observed in other ECG parameters.
There were no major changes observed in ECG parameters in Pool 2.
The Applicant’s Position:
Mirdametinib did not appear to have clinically meaningful adverse effects on ECG values during
any study.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment.

Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
A thorough QT/QTc study to evaluate the effect of mirdametinib and metabolite exposures on
cardiac repolarization in healthy participants (MEK-NF-102) was conducted. In that study, the
relationship between mirdametinib concentration and QTcF was successfully characterized
using a pre-specified linear mixed-effects model. No clinically significant effects of both
mirdametinib 6 and 14mg doses (approximately 2x and 6x the therapeutic exposure of
mirdametinib, respectively) were observed on the ECG.
Additionally, a search of the MedDRA Standardized MedDRA Queries “Torsades de pointes/QT
prolongation” plus the PT of seizure in the pooled clinical safety data was conducted. Five
pediatric and 5 adult participants with NF1 PN in Pool 1 were identified. All participants were
receiving mirdametinib 2 mg/m2 BID, with a maximum total daily dose of 4 mg BID. In Pool 1, 1
adult participant reported a non-serious TEAE of ECG QT prolonged. This participant had an
average baseline QTcF of 367.1 msec. On C1D15, their average QTcF was 404.92 msec,
representing an increase of 37.82 msec, when the event of electrocardiogram QT prolonged
was reported. The event resolved with the next triplicate ECG during the C2D15 visit (Day 42),
when the average QTcF was 369.17 msec. No events of torsades de pointes were reported.
In Pool 1, 2 pediatric and 2 adult participants reported TEAEs of seizure, including 1 serious
event in a pediatric participant. All 4 participants experiencing seizure had confounding medical
history, including seizure, epilepsy, complex partial seizures, or ongoing long-term use of
levetiracetam and clonazepam.
All other TEAEs from Pool 1 were non-serious; these included: 3 pediatric participants and
1 adult participant reporting events of syncope and 1 adult participant reporting an event of
electrocardiogram repolarization abnormality. All events of syncope were assessed as not
related to study treatment. One participant interrupted study treatment due to syncope; no
other participants modified study treatment as a result of syncope events. One participant
reported a related event of electrocardiogram repolarization abnormality on Day 15. The event
resolved with the next triplicate ECG on Day 42 with no change to study treatment.
In Pool 2, 10 adult participants with advanced cancers were identified with MedDRA PTs under
the Standardized MedDRA Queries for “Torsades de pointes/QT prolongation” plus the PT of

study drug discontinuation. All other events (cardiac arrest, loss of consciousness, ventricular
fibrillation, and ventricular tachycardia) occurred following discontinuation of study treatment
and in the context of the participant’s myocardial infarction. Importantly, this participant was
found to have a total occlusion of the left anterior descending artery, which was assessed as
not related to study treatment.
One additional participant reported cardiac arrest assessed as not related to study treatment.
This participant’s cause of death was listed as cancer and cardio-respiratory arrest due to
disease progression from their metastatic breast cancer.
One additional participant reported loss of consciousness on Day 4, 1 day after the TEAE of
renal failure and 3 days after the TEAE of hypoxia, both of which resulted in study drug
discontinuation. The participant died on Day 5 with cause of death cited as hypoxia, disease
progression, and renal failure. None of these events were assessed as related to study
treatment.
Two participants reported serious TEAEs of seizure. Both events were assessed as not related to
study treatment. One participant interrupted study treatment as a result of the event. The
second participant did not modify study treatment as a result of the event; this participant
subsequently died, with cause of death listed as disease progression due to malignant
melanoma.
One participant reported a TEAE of electrocardiogram repolarization abnormality resulting in
discontinuation from the study. Of note, this participant was found to have a T-wave
abnormality noted on the first of their triplicate baseline ECGs. This finding was then noted on
their triplicate ECGs on Day 1, along with non-specific ST-T changes, when the event was
reported. It was assessed as related to study treatment and study treatment was discontinued.
The Applicant’s Position:
In the thorough QT/QTc study, mirdametinib had no effect on QTc at doses of 6 and 14mg,
which resulted in approximately 2x and 6x the therapeutic exposures, respectively.

Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the

Reference ID: 5528369

NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
An analysis of all participants with RVO in Pools 1 and 2 showed that all participants reported at
least 1 predisposing factor for RVO, including hypertension, diabetes, hypercholesterolemia,
COVID-19 vaccination, and advanced cancer, as well as concomitant medications. However,
the role of mirdametinib cannot be excluded (Li 2023).
The microvascular abnormalities associated with NF1 may make this patient population
susceptible to RVO (Moadel 1994; Mori 2001). RVO has been described in clinical trials of MEK
inhibitors (Méndez-Martínez 2019). MEK inhibitors may block antiapoptotic proteins, block
natural retinal reactions to stress or oxidative injury, and prevent the repair of damaged retinal
pigment epithelial cells, ultimately leading to the accumulation of damage and the
degeneration of cells (van der Noll 2013). It has been shown that asymptomatic retinal changes
may be present in a large percentage of MEK inhibitor treated patients (van Dijk 2018) and
ocular toxicities with MEK inhibitors appear to be a class effect that is generally transient in
nature (Leijen 2012; LoRusso 201; van der Noll 2013; van Dijk 2018). Dose and dosing schedules
may be important factors for the risk of RVO in patients treated with MEK inhibitors
(van derNoll 2013). Individual characteristics that have not been fully characterized likely also
contribute to the risk for RVO.
The Applicant’s Position:
The available data, along with a plausible mechanism associated with MEK inhibition, support
recognizing RVO as an important identified risk for mirdametinib, and therefore proposed for
inclusion in the Warnings and Precautions of the label, though it should be noted that events in
patients with NF1 PN may be confounded by the microvascular abnormalities associated with
NF1 (Moadel 1994, Mori 2001).
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees with the Applicant’s incidence rates for retinal and visual AEs in Pool 1.
In the pooled safety population, ocular toxicity occurred in 25й ŽĨ ƉĂƚŝĞŶƚƐ treated with

Ejection fraction decreased is recognized as an identified risk for mirdametinib and therefore

measured decreases in ejection fraction were asymptomatic in the study participants, the

risk is considered important.

The Applicant’s Position:
Skin events, including dermatitis acneiform, a composite of non-acneiform rashes (including
rash, rash maculo-papular, rash pustular, rash erythematous, rash papular, exfoliative rash,
papule, rash macular, and rash pruritis), alopecia, hair texture changes, hair color changes, dry
skin, and pruritus, were reported at an increased incidence relative to that anticipated for the
patient population with NF1 PN. They are recognized as non-important identified risks as these
events were generally Grade 1 or 2 and did not frequently result in the discontinuation of study
treatment. However, dose modifications may be beneficial for patients; as such, skin toxicity is
proposed for inclusion in the Warnings and Precautions section of labeling.
The FDA’s Assessment:

in Pool 1. In the pooled safety population, rash occurred in 84й of patients; of these, 31й ǁĞƌĞ

rash, dermatitis, erythematous rash, palmar-plantar erythrodysaesthesia syndrome, exfoliative
rash, skin exfoliation, pruritic rash, papule, papular rash and macular rash. The most frequent

Other Noteworthy Adverse Events
The Applicant’s Position:
In addition to the skin events mentioned in Section 8.2.5.3, the following events are considered
non-important identified risks: diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, abdominal pain, constipation,
fatigue, blood CPK increased, alanine aminotransferase increased, aspartate aminotransferase
increased, and blood alkaline phosphatase increased. While these were reported at an
increased incidence relative to that anticipated for the patient population with NF1 PN, and so
are recognized as identified risks for mirdametinib, these events were generally Grade 1 or 2
and did not frequently result in the discontinuation of study treatment, hence they are
considered non-important.
Stomatitis was recognized as a non-important identified risk for the pediatric population and as
a potential risk in the adult population. In addition, the following risks were recognized as nonimportant identified risks in the pediatric population only: paronychia, eczema, and neutrophil
count decreased.
The FDA’s Assessment:
GI toxicity is a known MEK inhibitor class effect. FDA’s analysis of events of gastrointestinal
disorders in Pool 1 revealed that most adverse events of diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, abdominal
pain, constipation, and stomatitis were Grades 1 and 2 in severity. Grade 3 adverse events of
abdominal pain occurred in ϰй of adult patients; Grade 3 diarrhea and abdominal pain
occurred in ϱй ĂŶĚ ϯ͘ϰй ŽĨ pediatric patients, respectively. There were no Grade 4 adverse
events.
Based on FDA’s analysis of the safety data describing the observed GI toxicity in Pool 1, it was
not included in the Warnings and Precautions section of the mirdametinib USPI due to the very
low incidence of severe (Grade 3) events and the low incidence of events requiring
discontinuation of therapy.
Increased CPK is a class effect reported with other MEK inhibitors. In Pool 1, increased CPK
occurred in ϱϮй ŽĨ ƉĂƚŝĞŶƚƐ and ш Grade 3 events of increased CPK occurred in ϯ͘ϴй of patients.
Most of the events were Grade 1 or 2 in severity, with Grade 3 events occurring ŝŶ ϯ ;ϱйͿ
pediatric patients and a Grade 4 event occurring in ϭ ;ϮйͿ ƉĞĚŝĂƚƌŝĐ ƉĂtient. One ;ϭйͿ Grade 4
event was observed in an adult patient.

;ϲϳ͘ϳйͿ͕ ǁŚŝĐŚ ŝƐ ƐƵďƐƚĂŶƚŝĂůůǇ ůĞƐƐ ďŝŽůŽŐŝĐĂůůǇ ĂĐƚŝǀĞ ƚŚĂŶ ƚŚĞ ƉĂƌĞŶƚ ŵŝƌĚĂŵĞƚŝŶŝď͘ ,ĞŶĐĞ͕ ƚŚĞ
potential impact of renal impairment on the PK of mirdametinib is considered to be low.
No studies with mirdametinib were conducted in participants with altered hepatic function.
PopPK analyses suggest that mirdametinib PK is not expected to be altered in patients with mild
hepatic impairment. Mirdametinib is extensively metabolized, but to what extent this occurs in
the liver is unknown, and it is not a narrow therapeutic index drug.
At the maximum recommended dose of 4 mg BID, clinically significant QTc interval prolongation
was not observed, and the overall safety data do not support recognizing an effect on cardiac

Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
Extrinsic factors
The safety of mirdametinib was analyzed by geographic location, food intake, or use of tobacco
and alcohol. Although studies were conducted in the US in accordance with the principles of
ICH E5 R1 guidance, the results can be extrapolated to the European Union (EU) population as
NF1 PN is an orphan condition and there are no ethnic differences. There are no intrinsic or
extrinsic ethnic factors that will preclude the extrapolation of the results. Moreover, the
diagnosis and treatment of NF1 PN are similar in the US and EU.
No overall differences in safety were observed among participants as a result of food intake or
use of tobacco and alcohol.
The Applicant’s Position:
Overall, the observed differences among subgroups by age are not considered to have a clinical
impact on the safety profile of mirdametinib as they were most likely attributable to the age
difference between the groups and age-related development. As hepatic metabolism of
mirdametinib has not been studied, its use in patients with moderate and severe hepatic
impairment should be considered to have significant effects on the pharmacokinetics of
mirdametinib. Please see Section 6.2.1 for additional discussion of the effect of hepatic
impairment on mirdametinib PK.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment of safety subgroup analyses for age, sex, race,
ethnicity, BMI, and organ dysfunction in the adult and pediatric cohorts of Pool 1. Please refer
to Section 6.2.1 for a discussion on the effect of hepatic impairment on mirdametinib PK.
Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
The Applicant’s Position:
No studies have been conducted with mirdametinib specifically to address safety concerns.
Safety monitoring continues in the ongoing long-term follow-up phase of Study MEK-NF-201.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees and notes that the ReNeu study includes an optional Long-Term Follow-up
(LTFU) Phase with continued safety monitoring that is currently ongoing. Patients who
completed Cycle 24 of the Treatment Phase and did not meet withdrawal criteria were eligible
to participate in the LTFU.

The Applicant’s Position:
The safety and effectiveness of mirdametinib have been established in pediatric patients aged
шϮ years. The clinical data is not suggestive of an effect on bone as a result of mirdametinib
treatment. All the relevant events identified in Study MEK-NF-201 were confounded by a
variety of factors which are expected to have contributed to their events. The available clinical
data do not support recognizing bone effects, including fracture, as a risk for mirdametinib.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment. However, given the relevant bone toxicities
identified in the non-clinical data and the limitations associated with a small safety database, a
further assessment of growth and development parameters in pediatric patients will be
included as a PMR. Refer to Section 13.

in either of the two highest dose groups in Pool 2, in comparison to the same TEAEs in both
Pool 1 cohorts. Given that mirdametinib generally has a dose-proportional increases in
exposure in the dose range of 1 mg QD/BID to 30 mg BID, mirdametinib exposure in these two
Pool 2 dose groups represent approximately 2 to 8 times the maximum recommended NF1 PN
dose for mirdametinib of 4mg BID. Thus, data from the highest dose groups in Pool 2 may
represent possible outcomes for mirdametinib overdoses. However, dosing of mirdametinib in
participants in Pool 2 was sustained so the events observed may not necessarily represent
events that may occur in the setting of a single excessive dose of mirdametinib in a patient
receiving treatment with mirdametinib 4 mg BID or in the setting of accidental ingestion of
mirdametinib.
ǀĞŶƚƐ ǁŝƚŚ ĂŶ ŝŶĐŝĚĞŶĐĞ ŝŶ WŽŽů Ϯ ŽĨ Ăƚ ůĞĂƐƚ ĚŽƵďůĞ Žƌ хϮϬй ŚŝŐŚĞƌ ƚŚĂŶ ƚŚĞ ŝŶĐŝĚĞŶĐĞ ŝŶ ĞŝƚŚĞƌ

increased, hypoalbuminemia, decreased appetite, dehydration, confusional state, and anemia.
Hypoalbuminemia and anemia are unlikely to occur acutely following an acute overdose of
mirdametinib since changes in these laboratory parameters typically occur gradually (with the
exception of acute anemia due to blood loss).
Thirteen of these events are considered identified risks for mirdametinib at doses studied in
MEK-NF-201, and thus are not specific toxicities of an overdose. These events are diarrhea,
nausea, vomiting, constipation, abdominal pain, fatigue, rash, pruritis, dermatitis acneiform,
dry skin, ALT increased, AST increased, and ejection fraction decreased.

Drug abuse:
The potential for abuse was assessed by reviewing the psychiatric disorders SOC for TEAEs that
might suggest a psychoactive effect that could lead to drug abuse. In both Pool 1 and Pool 2,
there were no psychiatric TEAEs for euphoria, disinhibition, dissociation, or any other PTs that
might suggest a psychoactive effect that could lead to drug abuse.
Withdrawal and rebound:
Dependence was assessed by reviewing TEAE tables from Pool 1 and Pool 2. There were no
TEAEs for drug dependence, drug withdrawal, or other terms that might suggest mirdametinib
could cause physical dependence or symptoms of withdrawal.

The Applicant’s Position:
In general, the adverse reactions associated with a mirdametinib overdose are likely to be
ĐŽŶƐŝƐƚĞŶƚ ǁŝƚŚ ƚŚĞ ŬŶŽǁŶ ĂĚǀĞƌƐĞ ƌĞĂĐƚŝŽŶƐ Ăƚ ƌĞĐŽŵŵĞŶĚĞĚ ĚŽƐĞƐ ŽĨ чϰ ŵŐ /͕ ĂůďĞŝƚ
potentially more severe. Clinical data above 30 mg BID in adults and above 4 mg BID in
pediatrics are not available. No specific antidotes exist for the treatment of mirdametinib
overdose. Hemodialysis is unlikely to result in additional elimination as the estimated fraction
ƵŶďŽƵŶĚ ĨŽƌ ŵŝƌĚĂŵĞƚŝŶŝď ŝƐ Ϭ͘ϯй. Consequently, in the case of overdose, general supportive
and symptomatic care is recommended.
No potential for abuse or dependence has been identified for mirdametinib.

The FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment.

Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
The Applicant’s Position:
The safety population included in Study MEK-NF-201 and Pool 1 is representative of the
intended real-world population of patients with NF1 PN. The observed safety profile represents
a manageable safety profile for mirdametinib 2mg/mg2 BID for the treatment of adult and
pediatric patients 2 years of age and older with NF1 PN, and can be reasonably expected to
reflect the safety profile to be observed when used as indicated following marketing
authorization.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees with the description of the safety profile of mirdametinib provided by the
Applicant and refers to product labeling and FDA’s analysis of adverse events. See Section 13
for additional clinical postmarketing requirements (PMRs).

In Pool 1, there were 2 participants in the pediatric cohort of Study MEK-NF-201 who reported
Grade 4 events (blood creatine phosphokinase increased by 1 participant, and near drowning
and laryngeal edema by the second participant). In the adult cohort of Pool 1, there were
2 participants from Study MEK-NF-201 who reported Grade 4 events (COPD and pneumonia
bacterial, by 1 participant each).
There were no deaths on study in the pediatric cohort of Pool 1 or in the healthy volunteer
studies. The 1 death in an adult participant in Pool 1 due to COVID-19 was assessed by the
investigator as due to the infection and not to mirdametinib.

x The following events were recognized as identified risks because of the frequency of
their reports, temporal relationship to the start of treatment with mirdametinib, and
presence of a plausible biologic mechanism, and were deemed important due to their
potential impact on patients: ejection fraction decreased and retinal vein occlusion.

anticipated for the patient population with NF1 PN and are recognized as identified risks
as these events were generally Grade 1 or 2 and did not frequently result in the
discontinuation of study treatment: diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, abdominal pain,
constipation, skin events (including dermatitis acneiform, rash, pruritus, dry skin,
alopecia, hair color changes, and hair texture), blood CPK increased, alanine
aminotransferase increased, aspartate aminotransferase increased, blood alkaline
phosphatase increased, and fatigue.
The data presented support recognizing stomatitis as an identified risk for mirdametinib
in the pediatric population and as a potential risk in the adult population.
The data presented support recognizing paronychia, eczema, and neutrophil count
decreased, as identified risks for mirdametinib in the pediatric population only.
Given the low frequency and severity of events, the data support vision blurred and
detachment of retinal pigment epithelium being recognized as important potential risks.

Nonclinical data support embryo-fetal toxicity being recognized as an important potential risk
for mirdametinib, and mirdametinib should not be used during pregnancy. Mirdametinib had
no adverse effects on male and female rat fertility.
The following relevant observations were made, but they do not represent adverse clinical
findings: decreased hemoglobin, decreased leukocytes, lymphocyte count increased, increased
cholesterol, increased creatinine, decreased bicarbonate, hypo calcium corrected for albumin,
and increased glucose.
No clinically significant changes in urinalysis, vital signs, or ECG data were observed.
The most clinically impactful identified risks associated with the use of mirdametinib 2 mg/m2
BID in the treatment of NF1 PN and included in the Warnings and Precautions of the label are
retinal vein occlusion, ejection fraction decreased, dermatitis acneiform, and rash. Impactful
potential risks associated with the use of mirdametinib 2 mg/m2 BID in the treatment of NF1 PN
and included in the Warnings and Precautions of the label are vision blurred, detachment of
retinal pigment epithelium, and embryo-fetal toxicity. None of these events, nor any of the
other risks associated with the use of mirdametinib, in isolation or in aggregate, would preclude
the use of mirdametinib 2 mg/m2 BID for the treatment of patients with NF1 PN.
The Applicant’s Position:
The observed safety profile described in the integrated summary of safety represents a
manageable safety profile for mirdametinib 2 mg/m2 BID for the treatment of adult and
pediatric patients 2 years of age and older with NF1 PN. No aspects of the observed safety
profile would preclude the use of mirdametinib 2 mg/m2 BID for treating adult or pediatric

ĂƐƐĞƐƐĞĚ ďǇ /Z ŝŶ ƚŚĞ ƉƌŝŵĂƌǇ ĞĨĨŝĐĂĐǇ ƉŽƉƵůĂƚŝŽŶ ŽĨ ĂĚƵůƚ ƉĂƚŝĞŶƚƐ ǁĂƐ ϰϭй ;ϵϱй /͗ Ϯϵ͕ ϱϱͿ͖
ƚŚĞ ŵĞĚŝĂŶ KZ ǁĂƐ ŶŽƚ ƌĞĂĐŚĞĚ͘  ƚŽƚĂů ŽĨ ϴϴй ŽĨ ƌĞƐƉŽŶĚĞƌƐ ŚĂĚ Ă KZ ŽĨ шϭϮ ŵŽŶƚŚƐ ĂŶĚ
ϱϬй ŽĨ ƌĞƐƉŽŶĚĞƌƐ ŚĂĚ Ă KZ ŽĨ шϮϰ ŵŽŶƚŚƐ. While NF1-PNs are benign tumors, they carry
potential for severe associated morbidity including profound impact on quality of life, as
described by participants at FDA’s listening session for patients and caregivers with NF in June
2019 (see Section 2). In this context, FDA considered it critical that the ReNeu study
demonstrate unequivocal evidence of a treatment effect supported by data showing
improvement in physical function or an improvement in specific symptoms related to NF1-PN
disease that correlates with radiographic tumor shrinkage. Despite the limitations of
interpretation of the formal COA data, there was a general trend toward improvement in
symptoms associated with radiographic responses. FDA’s detailed review of patient-level
narratives for each responder, which included assessments of physical function, use of pain or
other symptom-ameliorating medications, as well as patient reported outcomes, provided clear
supportive evidence of the clinical benefit of tumor shrinkage. It is noted, however, that
narratives were not available for non-responders. FDA considers the totality of efficacy data to
represent a clinically meaningful treatment effect in patients with symptomatic, unresectable
NF1-PN and to constitute substantial evidence of effectiveness in this setting.
The safety profile of mirdametinib is acceptable for the intended population when considered
in the context of a serious and life-threatening disease. Important toxicities of mirdametinib
that were noted in this review include ocular toxicity, left ventricular dysfunction, dermatologic
adverse reactions, and embryo-fetal toxicity. These toxicities are addressed in the Warnings and
Precautions in the product label. Instructions for monitoring and dose modifications are
provided in the USPI.
Based on the evaluation of clinical data from the ReNeu study, the review team recommends
regular approval for mirdametinib for adult and pediatric patients 2 years of age and older with
neurofibromatosis type 1 (NF1) who have progressive or symptomatic plexiform neurofibromas
not amenable to complete surgical resection. In this study, mirdametinib demonstrated a
robust and clinically meaningful effect on ORR that is durable and associated with
improvements in disease-related morbidities. These factors represent direct clinical benefit to
patients and support regular approval of the drug.

Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)

10 Pediatrics
The Applicant’s Position:
This NDA is for a pediatric and adult indication. Data supporting the pediatric indication are
described throughout the application.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position. This NDA is exempt from the requirements under
Section 505B of the FD&C Act as amended by Title V of the FDA Reauthorization Act of 2017
(FDARA) because the marketing application is not intended for the treatment of an adult
cancer. Additionally, because the product has an orphan drug designation for this indication,
this NDA is exempt from the requirements to conduct pediatric assessments under the
Pediatric Research Equity Act (PREA). Therefore, an Initial Pediatric Study Plan (iPSP) is not
required for mirdametinib for the treatment of NF1-PN.
Given that there is limited long-term safety data for mirdametinib and a signal of growth delay
has been identified, the review team recommends a PMR to further characterize the potential
serious risk of long-term adverse effects of mirdametinib on growth and development in
pediatric patients (See Section 13).

12 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
The FDA’s Assessment:
There were no significant safety concerns identified during the NDA review requiring risk
management beyond labeling or warranting consideration for a Risk Evaluation and Mitigation
Strategy (REMS). Safety concerns are adequately addressed by information in the Warnings and
Precautions and Dosage and Administration sections of product labeling.

Population PK Analysis
19.4.2.1. Executive Summary
A PopPK model was developed to support the dose rationale for mirdametinib and is included
in this NDA. This activity included the following objectives:
x Develop fit-for-purpose PopPK models to characterize the PK of mirdametinib and its
active metabolite, PD-0315209, in healthy participants and participants with NF1 PN;
x Evaluate the effect of covariates on the variability of mirdametinib and PD-0315209 PK;
x Evaluate the magnitude and clinical relevance, when possible, of the statistically
significant findings from the analysis;
x Predict mirdametinib and PD-0315209 exposures following doses of mirdametinib under
scenarios of clinical importance.
Methods: Fit-for-purpose PopPK models were developed separately for mirdametinib and PD0315209. The PD-0315209 model was developed sequentially, using empirical Bayes estimates
(EBE) from the mirdametinib model. PD-0315209 PK was driven by fraction of parent drug
converted to metabolite (Fm) from the mass balance study (MEK-NF-101). Nonlinear mixed
effects modeling was employed to evaluate additional effects on model parameters and
improve the ability of the model to describe all available data. Covariate-parameter
relationships of interest were explored graphically and formally tested in each model if
substantial trends were evident. Numerical checks of parameter precision and model
robustness, goodness of fit (GOF) model diagnostics, and visual predictive checks (VPCs) were
used to qualify the models for use in simulations.

311
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5528369

NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
Results: Apparent clearance (CL/F) of mirdametinib was 4.17 L/h for a typical 75 kg participant.
At the median weight in pediatric participants (34.8 kg), CL/F of mirdametinib was 2.57 L/h. At
the median weight in adult participants (72.8 kg), CL/F of mirdametinib was 4.09 L/h. Apparent
clearance (CL/F) of mirdametinib was 4.17 L/h for a typical 75 kg participant. No difference in
mirdametinib CL/F was observed between healthy participants and patient populations.
Estimates for Vc/F, apparent peripheral volume of distribution – shallow compartment (Vp1/F)
and apparent peripheral volume of distribution - deep compartment (Vp2/F) were 16.0, 22.4
and 54.3 L, respectively. The PopPK model included allometric scaling of time-varying weight on
clearances and volumes of distribution. The estimated allometric coefficient for clearances was
0.631 and the estimate for volumes of distribution was 0.586.
The rapid absorption of mirdametinib following oral administration was described using a
transit compartment absorption model (TCAM) model, driven by a mean transit time (MTT) of
0.542 h and 2.46 transit compartments. No statistically significant differences in absorption
were found between fasted and unknown food status. However, administration of
mirdametinib with a high-fat meal resulted in increased variability in TCAM model parameters,
delayed absorption (MTT с ϯ͘Ϭϴ ŚͿ͕ ĂŶĚ ĂŶ ϴ͘ϴϬй ĚĞĐƌĞĂƐĞ ŝŶ ƌĞůĂƚŝǀe bioavailability (Frel). No
statistically significant differences were observed between the tablet and capsule formulations.
For PD-0315209, apparent clearance (CLm/F) was 0.583 L/h. At the median weight in pediatric
participants (34.85 kg), CLm/F of PD-0315209 was 0.328 L/h. At the median weight in adult
participants (72.8 kg), CLm/F of mirdametinib was 0.570 L/h. No statistically significant
difference in CLm/F was identified between healthy participants and patient populations. In
addition to allometric effects of weight on PD-0315209 clearances and volumes, the final
PD-0315209 PopPK model includes effects of CLcr on CLm/F and concomitant UGT2B7 inducer
use and age on Fm. While these effects were statistically significant when tested individually in
the model, the impact of each effect is limited relative to the overall variability of PD-0315209
exposures.
The applicant tested the effect of concomitant use of several inhibitors or inducers (e.g. CES1,
UGT1A6 and UGT2B7) through popPK analysis. The comedication analysis included the use of
multiple comedications and multiple classifications for the comedications used. The design of
the studies, in which multiple concomitant medications were allowed, along with the lack of
specificity of these medicines for a single enzyme, limits the precise characterization of enzyme
modulator interactions on the PK of mirdametinib using the PopPK analysis. Therefore, the
review team does not think popPK results alone can support the proposed DDI statement in the
label.
The sponsor also proposed unified dosing table to simplify the dosing regimen. The exposures
at steady state of unified dose are comparable to the previously tested tablets in the trial in
each BSA band based on the simulation. Therefore, the review team thinks the proposed
unified dosage is acceptable.

Overall Conclusions:
x Mirdametinib PK was adequately characterized by a three-compartment model with
absorption described by a TCAM.
x PD-0315209 PK was adequately characterized by a three-compartment model driven by
Fm with IIV on CLm/F, Vmc/F, Fm, Qm1/F, and Vmc/F.
x Mirdametinib and PD-0315209 exposure is linear with dose over the range of doses
evaluated (up to 20 mg).
x While a statistically significant effect for CLcr on CLm/F was identified, CL/F was not
impacted by CLcr and mild and moderate impairment were not associated with clinically
meaningful changes in exposure for mirdametinib and PD-0315209.

on the simulation.

19.4.2.2. PPK Assessment Summary
The Applicant’s Position:
The PK of mirdametinib was well described by a three-compartment model with central
clearance and a transit compartment absorption model. Covariates with significant but minor
impact included fed status on mean transit time and bioavailability, and body weight on
clearance and central volume. A three-compartment model was also used to describe the PK of
the metabolite PD-0315209. Covariates with significant but minor impact for the metabolite
included time-varying weight on clearance and central volume, creatinine clearance on
clearance, age on fraction metabolized, and concomitant use of UGT2B7 inducers on fraction
metabolized. All covariate effects were minor and none considered clinically relevant. There
was no relevant difference in PK between patients and healthy volunteers.

Exposure-Response Analysis
19.4.3.1. FDA’s ER (efficacy) Executive Summary
PKPDx models were developed to identify exposure-response relationships with TGI, best
overall response (BOR), duration of response (DOR), and time-to-progression (TTP). This activity
included the following objectives:
x Characterize the PKPDx relationships between mirdametinib dose/exposure and change
in tumor volume.
x Conduct population and trial simulations in support of dose justification and dose
optimization.
Methods: The TGI model was developed sequentially, beginning with a natural history model to
describe PN tumor growth in untreated participants from historical studies (01-C-022 and 08-C0079). Tumor volume measurements and PopPK-model predicted exposure parameters for
participants receiving mirdametinib in Studies NF-106 and MEK-NF-201 were included to
characterize the drug effect attributable to mirdametinib treatment. Covariate-parameter
relationships of interest were explored and structurally tested using stepwise covariate
325
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5528369

NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219379/219389
Mirdametinib (GOMEKLI)
modeling (SCM) and final models were qualified by visual predictive checks (VPCs) and
numerical checks of parameter precision and model robustness.
Bayesian post hoc pharmacokinetics (PK) parameter estimates were used to estimate
mirdametinib for subsequent exposure-response analysis. To assess the impact of changes in
exposure for DOR, BOR, and TTP exposure-response relationships were graphically evaluated in
all patients in MEK-NF-201 and NF-106. Covariate relationships were explored when the ER
relationship was found to be significant. BOR, DOR, and TTP were evaluated by logistic
regression or Cox regression analyses.
Results: Disease progression was best described by a Gompertz growth model with natural
decay. The natural history data (NHD) model showed effects of age and tumor volume baseline
on natural growth parameters. Participants aged 2-17 years old had higher natural growth rates
(KG) combined with lower tumor decay rates (KD) but a higher drug effect (KDrug). Adults (>17
years) were found to have a lower KG and higher KD but a lower KDrug. These factors led to a
similar change in tumor volume over time between the two age groups. A higher tumor
baseline was also linked to a higher maximum tumor volume (TSmax).
The NHD model was used as a basis for the TGI model to describe tumor growth in the absence
of treatment. When mirdametinib treated participants were included in the model, a linear
drug ĞĨĨĞĐƚ ŽĨ ŵŝƌĚĂŵĞƚŝŶŝď ŽŶ ƚƵŵŽƌ ŐƌŽǁƚŚ ǁŝƚŚ Ă ƐůŽƉĞ ŽĨ Ϭ͘ϭϴϵ ǁĞĞŬоϭͬ;ŶŐͬŵ>Ϳ ĚĞƐĐƌŝďĞĚ
the data best. The effect was estimated to diminish exponentially over time and this decay was
ϴϮ͘ϴй ůŽǁĞƌ in the pediatric population (2-17 years old) compared to adults, changing from
Ϭ͘ϭϳϮ ŵŽŶƚŚоϭ ŝŶ ĂĚƵůƚƐ ƚŽ Ϭ͘ϬϮϵϮ ŵŽŶƚŚоϭ ŝŶ ƚŚĞ ƉĞĚŝĂƚƌŝĐ ƉŽƉƵůĂƚŝŽŶ͘ dŚĞ ŚĂůĨ-life of the
decay of the drug effect in adults was estimated to be 4.03 months, compared to 23.7 months
in the pediatric population.
BOR was analyzed as a function of nominal mirdametinib maximum concentration at steady
state (Cmax,ss), mirdametinib minimum concentration at steady state (Cmin,ss), mirdametinib
area under the concentration vs. time curve at steady state within a dosing interval
(AUC0о12,ss). AUC0о12,ss and Cmin,ss were statistically significant predictors of BOR; Cmax,ss
was not a significant predictor of BOR. No statistically significant covariates were identified with
the relationship to AUC0-12,ss. No significant relationships were found between exposure
parameters and DOR or TTP.
Comparison of efficacy showed that probability of response and reduction in tumor growth
both increase with target dose and mirdametinib exposure. Longitudinal simulations suggest an
increasing and earlier rate of response as dose increases and a greater rate of response in adult
and adolescent subjects compared to children. All responses were associated with substantial,
overlapping prediction intervals (PIs). Based on a utility analysis of evaluating a smaller cohort
at a target dose of 1.5 mg/m2, the proďĂďŝůŝƚŝĞƐ ŽĨ ĂĐŚŝĞǀŝŶŐ ϱϬ͕ ϲϬ͕ Žƌ ϳϬй ƌĞƐƉŽŶƐĞ rates were
substantially higher for the 2 mg/m2 cohort than the 1.5 mg/m2 cohort in the full population.

19.4.3.3. FDA’s ER (safety) Executive Summary
PKPDx models were developed to identify exposure-response relationships with TEAEs. This
activity included the following objectives:
x Characterize the relationships between mirdametinib and PD-0315209 exposure and
safety response in NF1 PN participants.
x Conduct population and trial simulations in support of dose justification and dose
optimization.

Methods: Bayesian post hoc PK parameter estimates were used to estimate mirdametinib and
PD-0315209 exposures for subsequent exposure-safety analysis. To assess the impact of
changes in exposure for safety endpoints, exposure-response relationships were graphically
evaluated in all patients in MEK-NF-201 and NF-106. Covariate relationships were explored
when the ER relationship was found to be significant. TEAEs and time to first dose reduction
were evaluated by logistic regression or Cox regression analyses.
Results: Mirdametinib and/or PD-ϬϯϭϱϮϬϵ hϬоϭϮ͕ƐƐ ǁĞƌĞ ĨŽƵŶĚ ƚŽ ďĞ ƚŚĞ ďĞƐƚ ĞǆƉŽƐƵƌĞ
predictors for all significant TEAEs. Of the grade 1+ binary endpoints, the probability of an ALT
elevation, AST elevation, or CPK increase event were found to have statistically significant
relationships with mirdametinib exposure. The probability of acneiform rash, ALT elevation, and
AST elevation were found to have statistically significant relationships with PD-0315209
exposure. The similarity in TEAE ER relationships was driven by the strong correlation between
mirdametinib and PD- 0315209 as PD-0315209 exposure is a function of mirdametinib
exposure.
Age group (2-12, 12-17, >17) was a significant covariate for the probability of grade 1+ AST
elevation (mirdametinib and PD-0315209 models), grade 1+ CPK increase (mirdametinib
model), and grade 1+ acneiform rash (PD-0315209 model only). For grade 1+ AST elevation as a

children less than 12 years of age. The probability of grade 1+ CPK increase was shown to

time to first dose reduction.
Comparison of safety events showed that probability of incidence increases with target dose
and mirdametinib exposure; however, statistically significant ER relationships were only
observed for grade 1+ events. The predicted increase in probability of grade 1+ events is
unlikely to be clinically impactful.

19.4.3.4. ER (safety) Assessment Summary
The Applicant’s Position:
Exposure-response analyses were conducted using both the mirdametinib and PD-0315209
PopPK model to assess relationships with selected safety endpoints obtained from patient
Studies NF-106 and MEK-NF-201. Mirdametinib and/or PD-ϬϯϭϱϮϬϵ hϬоϭϮ͕ƐƐ ǁĞƌĞ ĨŽƵŶĚ ƚŽ
be the best exposure predictors for all significant TEAEs. Of the Grade 1+ binary endpoints, the
probability of an ALT elevation, AST elevation, or CPK increase event were found to have
statistically significant relationships with mirdametinib exposure. The probability of acneiform
rash, ALT elevation, and AST elevation were found to have statistically significant relationships
with PD-0315209 exposure. The similarity in TEAE ER relationships was driven by the strong
correlation between mirdametinib and PD- 0315209 as PD-0315209 exposure is a function of
mirdametinib exposure.
No ER relationships for Grade 2+ events were significant with mirdametinib or PD-0315209
exposure. No mirdametinib exposure-response relationships were found for dose reductions or
time to first dose reduction.
